Let me plan the hybrid approach.\n\nFloyd-Warshall: dist is column-major (idx2(i,j)=j*n+i, i.e., column i contiguous? idx2(i,j,n) = j*n+i so element (i,j) at j*n+i, meaning column-major: i varies fastest = row index within column j). The inner loop is over j with i outer. But accesses: dist[idx2(j,i)] = i*n+j => this is (j,i) with layout j? Wait idx2(a,b)=b*n+a. So idx2(j,i)=i*n+j, contiguous in j (inner loop is j), good. dist[idx2(k,i)] = i*n+k, constant wrt j (read). dist[idx2(j,k)] = k*n+j contiguous in j. So actual loop iterates over row i (source) and column j destination, and it's effectively standard dist[i][j] where index = i*n+j. So dist is row-major with row i contiguous? idx2(j,i)=i*n+j: first argument j = column, second i=row; row-major layout. So it's dist[row=i][col=j]. Fine.\n\nClassic Floyd-Warshall: for k: for i: for j: dist[i][j] = min(dist[i][j], dist[i][k]+dist[k][j]). Path update path[i][j]=k.\n\nParallelization approaches:\n- CUDA: One block/thread per (i,j), looping over k inside or launching kernel per k. For maximum performance, blocked Floyd-Warshall with shared memory tiling is standard. Given GPU with large memory, for n up to maybe 4096 or 8192, running whole matrix on one GPU is fine. But hybrid MPI: partition rows or columns across MPI ranks and GPUs. Need correctness including path matrix.\n\nA common hybrid parallel Floyd-Warshall: 2D block decomposition across MPI ranks (row and column blocks), and each rank uses CUDA on local submatrix for block computations, OpenMP for CPU parts / data movement? Hmm. Need to be careful about complexity and correctness.\n\nAlternative simpler hybrid: MPI ranks each own a contiguous block of rows (i range). At each k, the k-th row (dist[k][*]) must be broadcast to all ranks (all need dist[i][k] and row k values). Each rank updates its local rows i and all j. Within rank, CUDA computes the local rows update. OpenMP could parallelize... but if GPU handles it, OpenMP is redundant. To genuinely combine all three, use MPI for inter-node, OpenMP for intra-node multi-GPU / CPU, CUDA for GPU compute. \"as appropriate for the problem.\" Maybe use MPI to distribute across nodes/ranks, OpenMP to manage threads, CUDA kernels for compute. Could use one GPU per rank, with MPI ranks per GPU. OpenMP used to parallelize host-side initialization/validation/packing, or to drive multiple CUDA streams? Hmm.\n\nThe requirement: \"hybrid approach combining MPI, OpenMP, and CUDA as appropriate\". It should unconditionally use specified parallelization approach. So all three must be genuinely used.\n\nDesign:\n- MPI: distribute rows of dist and path across ranks. Each rank gets rows [rowStart, rowEnd). Also all ranks need column k (dist[i][k] for local i) and row k (dist[k][j]) at each k.\n- CUDA: each rank computes local row block update on GPU using a kernel. Row-block update: local dist rows i in block, all j. For each k: kernel computes for each local i,j. Need dist[i][k] local (in row i), and dist[k][j] (row k). For np=2, 2841s vs 5299s; compute distribution dominates. Fine.\n\nBut can we improve kernel to be faster? Let's profile n=8192 np=1: 5299ms. Communication overhead: n kernel launches, each maybe 5us => 41ms; D2H/H2D+Bcast root each k ~ maybe 20us => 164ms. Total ~205ms, compute ~5.1s. Kernel effective bandwidth: dist read = n^3*4 = 2.2TB; writes when improvements: early iterations many writes maybe significant. Path reads always (4 bytes each) because we always load path before checking. That doubles traffic! We load path for every element regardless of whether any improvement. That's n^3*4 = 2.2TB. Total ~4.4TB / 5.1s = 863 GB/s, near peak. So the path vector load is costly. Could avoid loading path unless a change occurs in some lane. But we need path values only for changed lanes; we can defer loading path until after computing changed mask:\n\n```\nunsigned mask = ...;\nif (mask) {\n  uint4 p = *reinterpret_cast<uint4*>(path+base+j);\n  if(mask&1)p.x=k; ...\n  store path\n}\n```\nThis avoids path read for vector where no changes. Early in FW, many changes; later few. Could improve average. Let's implement: compute d and changed per component, only load p if changed. But current code loads p before. Modify.\n\nAlso, we could avoid loading rowk from global for every row by shared memory? rowk is cached, not major.\n\nAlso, dist write: only store if changed. We already. So traffic = dist read always (2.2TB) + path read/writes only changes + dist writes on changes. At later k, few changes. This could bring kernel closer to 2.2TB/5s=440GB/s? Wait current 4.4TB includes path read 2.2 and dist read 2.2. Without path read mostly, could nearly 2x for later iterations. But early changes still path. Overall improvement maybe 20-40%.\n\nLet's implement deferred path load. Also perhaps use `__ldg` for rowk. Compiler with const __restrict__ uses read-only cache automatically.\n\nAlso, consider using shared memory for rowk and dist column? Standard optimization: Each block could load rowk[j..j+tile] into shared once and process multiple rows i (grid.y loop). Our kernel loops rows within the block: for each block (j-range, starting row), loops i across all rows assigned to grid.y. It reads rowk for each i again! Actually inside the row loop, it loads `r` (rowk uint4) each iteration. Since rowk segment for this block is constant across i, we should hoist rowk load outside the i loop. That would reduce rowk traffic by factor of rows/grid.y? But grid.y is min(rows,65535). For rows <=65535, grid.y=rows, so each block handles exactly one row; no reuse. Wait gridY = rows if rows<=65535, so blockIdx.y=i and loop executes once. So no row reuse. For n up to 8192, rows<=8192, so every block one row. rowk segment loaded per row block => n^2 per k, cached in L2. Fine.\n\nBut if we instead reduce gridY and let each block process multiple rows, we could load rowk once per block then loop many rows, reducing L2 traffic but not main dist traffic. Then other ranks can receive row k+1 while still computing k (if they post receive early), and start k+1 once done. This enables overlap of communication with compute! This adds a kernel launch per iteration but allows overlap. Complex and gains maybe limited.\n\n- Use a different distribution: replicate the row needed for broadcast? Hmm.\n\n- Reduce number of communication iterations by blocking k: This is the standard way. Communicate B rows per k-block, and process B k's per phase. For batching, we need each rank to have the full k-block rows for all B iterations. But row k inside block gets updated during earlier k' in the same block, so a snapshot of all B rows before the block is not enough (except diagonal-safe? For k'<k, row k may improve via k'). Need intra-block updates. In blocked algorithm, pivot rows are updated within the block on every rank? If all ranks have a copy of the entire k-block rows (B x n), they can update those pivot rows locally within the block without communication until block end, because pivot rows only depend on pivot rows and columns. Actually the standard blocked algorithm: Phase 1 computes diagonal block (pivot block) on the rank(s) owning it; Phase 2 updates pivot rows/columns; Phase 3 updates rest. Distribution of full rows complicates. But if each rank keeps a full copy of the k-block rows (B x n), it could locally simulate updates to those rows during the block, and use them for updating its own rows; then at block end no need to sync? Let's think.\n\nFor each k in [K, K+B):\n- To update all rows using row k and column k, need current row k and column k.\n- If every rank has a copy of the B pivot rows (rows K..K+B-1), then for k within block, the pivot rows evolve due to earlier k in block. All ranks can update their copy of the B pivot rows locally (they have full rows including columns). So they can maintain current pivot rows without communication during block.\n- But column k entries for non-pivot rows (dist[i][k] for i outside pivot block) are local to each rank; the update formula needs dist[i][k] local, which is available. It does not affect pivot rows' evolution (pivot row k update uses dist[k][j] and dist[k][k]? Actually row k update for all j uses dist[k][k] + row k; only row k and column? Standard FW update for row k uses row k itself, dist[k][k]=0, so no change. Pivot rows for future k' within block are updated using row k'? Wait updating row r (pivot) at iteration k uses dist[r][k] + row k. Both r and k are pivot rows, so only pivot-row copies needed. Thus all ranks can maintain pivot rows locally using only the pivot-row matrix B x B/n. Yes! For the rest of their local rows, they need row k from pivot copy and local column k. So no communication needed during the block, as long as at block start all ranks have the B pivot rows current as of before block. Then each rank uses its local copy of pivot rows, updates them locally in the same way as the full algorithm, and uses them to update its own rows. At the end of the block, the pivot rows are current globally. Need be careful with initialization and pivot buffers.\n\nLet's modify `floydWarshall` function and allocate d_pivot, d_pivotPath, h_pivot in main.\n\nNew signature:\n```\nvoid floydWarshall(unsigned int* d_dist, unsigned int* d_path,\n                   const size_t numNodes, const size_t rows, const size_t rowStart,\n                   const int rank, const int size,\n                   unsigned int* d_pivot, unsigned int* d_pivotPath,\n                   unsigned int* h_pivot) \n```\nNo need d_row anymore; rowk is pointer into d_pivot.\n\nImplementation:\n```\nconstexpr size_t K_BLOCK = 64;\nconst bool vec4 = (numNodes %4==0);\n...\nfor (size_t kStart=0; kStart<numNodes; kStart += K_BLOCK) {\n    const size_t kEnd = std::min(kStart+K_BLOCK, numNodes);\n    const size_t pivotCount = kEnd - kStart;\n    // gather pivot rows\n    // local intersection for this rank\n    size_t sendStart, sendEnd; // global rows within [kStart,kEnd)\n    size_t a,b; rowRange(numNodes, rank, size, a,b);\n    size_t interStart = std::max(a,kStart);\n    size_t interEnd = std::min(b,kEnd);\n    size_t sendCount = interEnd>interStart ? (interEnd-interStart)*numNodes : 0;\n    if (sendCount>0) {\n       cudaMemcpy(h_pivot + (interStart-kStart)*numNodes,\n                  d_dist + (interStart-rowStart)*numNodes,\n                  sendCount*sizeof(unsigned), D2H);\n    }\n    // counts/displs\n    for p...\n    MPI_Allgatherv(rank==? ..., sendCount, MPI_UNSIGNED,\n                   h_pivot, counts, displs, MPI_UNSIGNED, MPI_COMM_WORLD);\n    cudaMemcpy(d_pivot, h_pivot, pivotCount*numNodes*sizeof(unsigned), H2D);\n    for (size_t k=kStart;k<kEnd;++k) {\n       const unsigned *rowk = d_pivot + (k-kStart)*numNodes;\n       launch local kernel with rowk\n       launch pivot kernel with rowk on d_pivot,d_pivotPath,pivotCount\n    }\n}\n```\nFor Allgatherv send buffer: if sendCount=0, pass h_pivot (non-null) with count 0.
